1	# Email triage and newsflow redesign: plan
2	
3	Oct 8, 2026 · @Kelvin
4	
5	This is a build spec to be used by a coding agent. The prototype classifies each of 300 synthetic emails by how much attention it needs, then compares the relevant ones with a five-company book and suggests changes for an investment analyst to decide on.
6	
7	## Overview
8	
9	The design is settled and nothing is built yet. The hand-off is this spec, the corpus prompt and six generation prompts. The corpus, the seed files, the criteria files and all code are still to be produced.
10	
11	### Method
12	
13	&#91;embedded content: proposed method · corpus and book in, brief and evaluation out\]
14	
15	Jev reads every email against the desk's relevance criteria and never sees the book. Two pathways follow the gate: attention notes for emails that need a person, and the analysis agent for emails that bear on a thesis. The analyst's decision is the only path by which the book changes.
16	The purpose of this is to automatically triage incoming emails for relevent research insights, identify those emails, and then parse research insights from the to be presented to an analyst.
17	
18	## Start here
19	
20	Build four things, in this order of importance:
21	
22	1. **A pipeline** that reads 300 synthetic emails and emits redundancy flags, triage labels, attention notes and thesis suggestions.
23	2. **A state layer:** criteria text files that users can read and extend, plus a driver model and a thesis for each of f
... [44534 more characters omitted]

[2026-10-09T06:13:45] TOOL RESULT
1000	- **Edit.** Change the strength or the wording of a new pillar, then accept.
1001	- **Dismiss.** The suggestion closes and stays visible in the company's history.
1002	- **Set conviction.** On a conviction review, the analyst picks a conviction from 1 to 5 or dismisses the review. The app never proposes the value.
1003	- **Verify.** An agent checks the claim against cached filings and returns a cited verdict.
1004	
1005	### Validation in stage 7
1006	
1007	- **Unknown item:** a claim ID is not one of the email's claims, a pillar ID is outside the candidate set recomputed from those claims' tickers, a new thesis names a ticker outside them, or a new pillar names a driver of another company. Reject.
1008	- **Quote mismatch:** a section is not found verbatim after whitespace normalization. Reject.
1009	- **Duplicate thesis:** a new-thesis candidate's statement reaches the duplicate threshold against an existing pillar, using the stage 2 embedding model. Reject.
1010	- **Monitor only:** every linked email is labeled monitor. Set strength to 1, and reject a new-thesis candidate.
1011	- **Out of bounds:** a stated figure falls outside the driver's bounds, or names a driver the pillar is not linked to. Keep the suggestion and drop the figure.
1012	- **Wrong period:** the claim's period is missing, or is not exactly the model's fiscal year, such as FY2027. Keep the suggestion and drop the figure.
1013	- **Second look:** no linked email is labeled thesis\_relevant or monitor. Kee

"""The web app: FastAPI + Jinja + HTMX, rendered on the server.

    TRIAGE_DATA_DIR=tests/fixtures/out uv run uvicorn triage_app.web.main:app

Every page is a GET that returns HTML. Every action is a POST that returns an HTMX fragment
and changes only the visitor's session (a POST without HTMX redirects back). Stage files are
read from `TRIAGE_DATA_DIR` (default `data/out/day_1`); the book is the seed plus the
session's change log, folded on every request and never stored. Every page carries the
synthetic-data banner. A quarantined email shows its sender and subject only, everywhere.

"""Read-only view of one set's stage files for the app.

The directory comes from `TRIAGE_DATA_DIR` (default `data/out/day_1`). The emails are not a
stage file: they are read from the set's corpus file through the corpus loader (label-free,
bodies exactly as in the corpus). `data/out/<set>` maps to `data/corpus/<set>/emails.jsonl`,
any other directory to the `emails.jsonl` beside it (the fixtures: `tests/fixtures/out` ->
`tests/fixtures/emails.jsonl`), and `TRIAGE_EMAILS_FILE` overrides both. Files are read once
and reloaded when any of them changes on disk. A missing file reads as empty, so the app
answers before every stage has run. Nothing here writes.
"""

import os
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from markupsafe import Markup, escape
from pydantic import BaseModel

from triage_app import config
from triage_app.pipeline.io import normalize_ws, read_list, read_one
from triage_app.schema import (
    AnalysisRecord, AttentionNote, Brief, Claim, CriteriaHistoryEntry, Email, EmailResult,
    EvalReport, LinkedSection, RedundancyRecord, Suggestion, TriageRecord, UsageReport,
)

DEFAULT_DATA_DIR = config.OUT_DIR / "day_1"


def data_dir_from_env() -> Path:
    raw = os.environ.get("TRIAGE_DATA_DIR", "").strip()
    if not raw:
        return DEFAULT_DATA_DIR
    path = Path(raw)
    return path if path.is_absolute() else config.ROOT / path

[2026-10-09T06:14:30] ASSISTANT
I've read SPEC.md in full, along with CLAUDE.md, DECISIONS.md, REVIEW.md, the Dockerfile and all of `src/triage_app/web/`. The app is running in the browser pane on day_1.

## Where the front end stands

**It already works end to end.** The previous build covered every screen and route in the spec:

- **Code:** [main.py](src/triage_app/web/main.py) (571 lines, FastAPI) does all the work. [data.py](src/triage_app/web/data.py) reads the stage files and reloads them when they change. [session.py](src/triage_app/web/session.py) keeps each visitor's changes in memory with rate limits. [runner.py](src/triage_app/web/runner.py) drives the live route, verify and "this mattered".
- **Screens:** Brief, Suggestion, Company, Criteria, Audit, Inbox, Email, Live, Eval and Monitor all render. Accept, edit, dismiss, verify, driver update, conviction, undo and reset are wired through HTMX fragments.
- **Spec rules already enforced:**
  - Every page carries the synthetic banner.
  - A quarantined email shows its sender and subject only, everywhere.
  - Opposite stances on one pillar show together on one card.
  - The live route is capped by session and across the whole app.
  - Errors show only the exception type, never its message.

**What's weak is the presentation.** It's 67 lines of CSS and plain panels:

- The nav is a flat row of 13 links that wraps into three lines on narrow screens.
- There's no visual hierarchy on the Brief. Alerts, cards and lists all look alike.
- The suggestion screen, which the spec calls "the center of the demo", stacks into one column and hides the linked emails below the fold.
- Numbers, probabilities and stances are shown as bare text and pills, and there's no dark mode.

**Constraints the spec fixes for any redesign:**

- Python only: server-rendered Jinja plus HTMX. No TypeScript and no front-end build step.
- One container serves the pages and the live route.
- Every screen says the data is synthetic.
- The app never shows a quarantined body and never proposes a number, trade or size.

**Loose ends I noticed (not front-end):**

- `data/out/day_1` has no `eval.json` yet, so the Eval page reads "No eval.json". `day_2` only has its output through `claims.json`.
- There's an uncommitted `jupyter` dependency in `pyproject.toml`. It would end up in the deployed image, so it should probably go in the dev group.
- I added `.claude/launch.json` so the app can be previewed here.

Two decisions will shape the work, so I'd rather ask than guess.

def eps_text(ticker: str, values: dict[str, float] | None = None) -> str:
    model = next(m for m in load_seed().models if m.ticker == ticker)
    if values:
        model = model.model_copy(deep=True)
        for d in model.drivers:
            d.analyst = values.get(d.id, d.analyst)
    return f"{compute(model)['analyst'].eps:,.2f}"


def eps_cells(page: str) -> list[str]:
    return re.findall(r'<td class="num eps">([^<]+)</td>', page)

[2026-10-09T06:28:45] ASSISTANT
I've restyled the whole web app after your reference, on the spec's stack: FastAPI with server-rendered templates and HTMX, no TypeScript and no build step. All 160 tests pass, `mypy --strict` is clean, and I clicked through accept, dismiss, row selection and the inbox filters in the browser. Nothing is committed yet.

**What changed:**
- **Header and tabs.** "Research desk" title with tabs underneath, and a company picker on the right. Your "Manage rules" button opens Criteria, and "Prepare research" became **Run a live email**.
- **Stat cards** with icons on Brief, Inbox, Audit, Company, Eval and Monitor.
- **List on the left, detail on the right** for Brief, Inbox and Audit, as in your mock:
  - The left side has grouped, collapsible lists with sender initials, times and label tags.
  - Clicking a row loads the email or suggestion into the right-hand side without a page reload. No new routes were added.
  - Accept and dismiss work right there, which covers the spec's "accept or dismiss inline" on the Brief.
  - Inbox gets search, a label filter and sub-tabs (All, Passed, Needs attention).
- **Suggestion screen.** The suggested change is on one side and the linked emails with their quotes on the other. It uses your reference's layout: rows of label and value, a green "Why the model suggests it" box, and full-width Accept and Dismiss buttons.
- **Email detail.** Jev's label, gate, signal-score bar, attention note and the code-written reason, with all 14 of Jev's answers as bars in an expandable section.
- **Other screens:**
  - Company has tables with the analyst-vs-consensus gap coloured, and a card for each pillar.
  - Criteria has a file list down the side and numbered rules.
  - Eval marks each measure as met or missed against its target, with a coloured confusion table.
- **Dark mode** follows the system setting, and phone widths stack into one column.

Every spec rule on screen is unchanged: the synthetic banner on every page, quarantined emails showing sender and subject only, opposite stances on one pillar shown together and neither preselected, and no number proposed by a model.

**Fixes and choices along the way:**
- **Accept bug.** Accept and Dismiss briefly deleted their own buttons when used in the right-hand side; that's fixed.
- **Stale styles.** Stylesheet links now carry a version stamp, so a new deploy never serves an old cached stylesheet. In dev, CSS edits only show after a `.py` file changes, because the stamp is computed at startup.
- **Eval targets.** These now live in `thresholds.py` as `EVAL_TARGETS`, keeping the repo's rule that every number is defined there once. Both choices are logged in `DECISIONS.md` (#56–57).

**Problems I found outside the UI:**
- **The tuned gate is stopping relevant emails.** `tuned/thresholds.json` sets the pass threshold to **0.98**. On day_1, 3 of 21 thesis_relevant emails are stopped, including one at signal score 0.97 (`synthetic_000001`). That fails the spec's 100% gate-recall target. It needs a look at the tuning, not the UI.
- **Missing outputs.** day_1 has no `eval.json`, so the Eval page shows an empty state. day_2's run stops after `claims.json`.
- **`jupyter` in the main dependencies.** The uncommitted `pyproject.toml` change adds it there, so it would ship in the deployed container. It belongs in the dev group.

The app is running in the preview pane, and I added `.claude/launch.json` so it can be started again. Should I commit this on a branch, or do you want to adjust the look first?

[2026-10-09T06:41:38] ASSISTANT
All displayed text across the site is now properly capitalized and readable. All tests pass, `mypy --strict` is clean, and I checked the screens in light mode at desktop width.

**How it works:** one new file, [labels.py](src/triage_app/web/labels.py), holds the display name for every value a person reads. The templates use it through a few filters, so no page formats a label by hand. The pipeline's output files on disk are unchanged; the conversion happens only when a page is drawn.

**What changed on screen:**
- **Labels and tags:** `thesis_relevant` reads "Thesis relevant" and `low_value` reads "Low value". Gate shows "Passed", "Stopped" or "Quarantined". Stance shows "Supports" or "Contradicts", status "Open" or "Accepted". Email types read "Sell-side research", "Meeting request" and so on. Note actions read "Reply" or "Attend", and topics "Macro" or "Sector".
- **Stage names** follow the spec's stage table, on Monitor and in the live trace: "Check for repeats", "Label and gate", "Extract claims", "Merge and rank".
- **Eval and criteria measures:** "Gate recall", "Monitor recall at the gate", "Ticker F1", "Meeting-like share". Missing review scores show "Pending hand review" and "N/A".
- **Text written by code** (gate reasons, rejection reasons, trace details) now reads as sentences. For example, "Thesis relevant 0.80; signal score 0.97 is below 0.98" and "Quarantined: instructs an AI 0.97 reaches 0.65". Email IDs inside that text are left as written.
- **Messages and errors** start with a capital and end with a period, for example "Strength must be 1, 2 or 3." Live-trace counts are pluralized correctly ("1 claim", "2 suggestions").
- **IDs** such as `NVDA.p2`, `synthetic_000005`, driver IDs and the criteria version stay exactly as written, shown as small grey monospace chips so they read as identifiers.
- **Spacing and small fixes:**
  - Chips inside section headings no longer turn all-caps.
  - The linked-email tag reads "Emails 1, 2" instead of "1 , 2".
  - The pane's "Open" button is renamed "Full page", so it no longer sits next to an "Open" status pill.
  - Monitor's latency columns read "p50 (ms)" and "Max (ms)".
  - Unit cells show "%" and "$bn".

About 20 assertions in `tests/test_web.py` checked the old raw wording, such as "second look" and `<td>extract</td>`. I updated them to the new wording; each still checks the same behaviour. The choice is recorded in `DECISIONS.md` (#58).

Still uncommitted, along with the redesign. Want me to commit both on a branch?

The attention emails now have their own **Needs attention** tab, next to Morning brief, with a count badge (20 on day 1). All tests pass, including a new one for this tab, and `mypy --strict` is clean.

**What's on the tab:**
- **Stat cards:** total requests, meeting requests (16), replies due today (6), and events and other requests (4).
- **Sub-tabs** split it by type: All requests, Meeting requests, Event invitations and Other. The type comes from Jev's email-type answer. A search box matches person, firm, ticker or subject.
- **Timeline grouping.** Cards are grouped into Due today, Due tomorrow, Due later and No deadline stated, soonest first.
- **Each card shows:**
  - **Who is requesting:** a "Requested by" label with the person's initials, their name, then their role and **firm**, taken from the sender line ("Name, Role, Firm").
  - **What they're offering:** the attention note's first sentence in **bold**, with the rest of the summary in grey beneath it, and the email's subject below that.
  - **Timeline:** a coloured line such as "Reply by Today, 5:00 PM" (red for today, amber for tomorrow, blue for later), the action asked for, and when the email arrived.
  - **Relevance tags:** ticker chips that link to the company page, topic tags, the triage label, and Jev's attention probability.
- **Clicking a card** opens the full email on the right, with its attention note, classification and highlighted body.

The Brief keeps its own "Needs your attention" section, which the spec orders by attention probability. It now links to the new tab, and the Brief's "Needs your attention" stat card opens it too.

**Two things to know:**
- **This is a new route.** The spec's route table has no `GET /attention`. It only reads existing outputs and adds no actions, so no contract changes; I recorded it in `DECISIONS.md` (#59).
- **"Other" requests are mixed.** One of the "Other" items is sell-side research that asks you to attend a call: Jev typed it as research, but it still needs a person. The card shows its real type rather than forcing it into Meeting requests.

[2026-10-09T07:01:35] ASSISTANT
The inbox's label filter works now, and all four recommendations are built. All tests pass, including new ones for each new screen, and `mypy --strict` is clean. I checked each screen at desktop width; Review was also checked at phone width.

**Inbox label filter.** The script was flagging filtered rows as hidden, but a style rule I'd written kept drawing them anyway. My earlier check only counted the flags, so I missed it. Choosing "Monitor" now leaves exactly the 66 Monitor emails on screen. The same fix makes the search box and sub-tabs actually hide rows, on every list page.

**1. Navigation.** The main tabs are now Morning brief, Review, Needs attention (with its count) and Inbox. The fifth tab, Book, now opens the new overview. Audit, Criteria, Live, Eval and Monitor sit under an **Under the hood** menu, each with a one-line description. Undo, Reset and "Run a live email" stay in the header.

**2. Book (`/book`).**
- Stat cards: longs, shorts, open suggestions, and changes made this session.
- A positions table: stance and size, conviction as dots, and EPS and target price with their gap to consensus in green or red. The open-suggestion count links to Review.
- A card per company listing its pillars. Each pillar shows its accepted evidence and the open suggestions on it as Supports/Contradicts pills, plus "“Wrong if” met" when a test is met; each pill opens that item in Review.

**3. Review (`/review`).** You step through every suggestion in brief order.
- The queue is on the left, with a status dot per item. A "0 of 28 decided" progress bar and Previous/Next buttons sit on top.
- The middle and right show the same detail as the suggestion page, so accept, dismiss, edit and verify all work as before.
- Keys: **A** accepts, **D** dismisses, **J/K** or the arrow keys move between items. After you decide, the next open suggestion opens on its own.
- I tested this live: pressing A on NVDA.p1 counted 1 of 28 and moved to NVDA.p2. I then undid that accept, so your session is back at zero.
- When everything is decided, a summary card links to the Book.

**4. Calendar on Needs attention.** A List/Calendar toggle sits at the top. The calendar has one column per reply-due day (Today in red, then Tomorrow, Thursday Oct 15, …), plus a "No deadline" column. Each card shows the time and action, the person and firm, the offer in bold, and ticker tags. These are reply deadlines; the meeting itself is often later, and the page says so.

**One data change you should know about:** the day_1 output files were rewritten at 23:45 by a pipeline run outside this work. The brief went from 15 suggestions to 28, and the app reloaded on its own, so every screen now shows 28.

The choices are recorded in `DECISIONS.md` (#61). Everything is still uncommitted; say when you'd like it committed on a branch.

All four changes are in. Tests pass, `mypy --strict` is clean, and I checked the suggestion and audit screens.

**1. Plain-English labels.** They're set once in `labels.py`, so every tag, filter, chart, sentence and the Eval tables use them:

| Was | Now |
|---|---|
| thesis_relevant | **Actionable** |
| monitor | **Worth watching** |
| redundant | **Already known** |
| low_value | **Low priority** |
| irrelevant | **Not relevant** |
| human_attention | **Needs a person** |

The code-written reasons read naturally too, for example "Actionable 0.80; signal score 0.97 is below 0.98". The values stored in the pipeline's files haven't changed.

**2. "Wrong if" removed.** The test text no longer appears on the suggestion, company, Book or brief cards, or in the edit form. Editing a new pillar still saves its original test. One judgement call: the alert raised when a test is met now says **"Pillar at risk"**. If you'd rather drop those alerts entirely, I can.

**3. "Linked emails labeled" removed** from the suggestion detail.

**4. Capitalization.** Section headings and table headers are sentence case now instead of ALL CAPS. I then scanned every page's rendered text for anything starting lowercase and fixed the misses:
- "vs consensus" became "Gap to consensus".
- "n/a" became "N/A".
- Monitor's "p50/p95" became "Median" and "95th percentile".
- The Review shortcut hints are capitalized.
- The Audit cards read "Already known" and "Low priority or not relevant".

Two things in the scan I deliberately left alone. Email subjects and quoted passages appear exactly as the sender wrote them, since quotes must match the email word for word. Model IDs are identifiers and stay as written.

This is recorded in `DECISIONS.md` (#62). Everything is still uncommitted.

def effective_entries(log: list[LogEntry]) -> list[LogEntry]:
    """Entries still in effect: not cancelled by a later undo, and not undos themselves."""
    cancelled: set[str] = set()
    for entry in reversed(log):
        if entry.id in cancelled:
            continue
        if entry.reverses is not None:
            cancelled.add(entry.reverses)
    return [e for e in log if e.id not in cancelled and e.reverses is None]

    theses = {t.ticker: t.model_copy(deep=True) for t in seed.theses}
    models = {m.ticker: m.model_copy(deep=True)

All six requests from this round are done. The full test suite passes, including new tests for each change, and `mypy --strict` is clean across `src/` and `tests/`. I checked the pages in the browser and reset the session after testing.

**1. Scores hidden behind "View scores," and Jev unnamed.**
- Each email shows its label, analysis outcome, email type, companies and topics, plus a short "What happened" sentence with no numbers (for example, "Classified as worth watching. Stopped before analysis.").
- Everything numeric now sits in a collapsed **View scores** panel: the signal score, the code-written decision record, every classifier probability and the repeat similarity scores. It's on the email detail, on each Audit row and in the live trace.
- The "Attention 0.96" tags are gone from the Brief and Needs attention.
- "Jev" now reads "the classifier" in all page text. The Monitor page (under the hood) still lists the model ID `jev-1.13.0` as a fact about the run.

**2. Editing pillars by hand.** You approved the contract change. Two new kinds of change (pillar edited, pillar removed) are tracked in the session log, and `DECISIONS.md` #64 has the details. On each company page:
- Each pillar card has **Edit** (wording and linked assumptions), **Log evidence** (supports or contradicts, strength 1–3) and **Remove** (with a confirmation).
- An **Add a pillar** card sits at the end.
- Assumption values can be edited in the table; press Enter and EPS and target price recompute. I tested this: iPhone growth 1.0 → 6.0 took EPS from $7.27 to $7.45.
- Conviction can be set from the page header.
- Everything is undoable and stays within the session; the seed files are untouched. A removed pillar's number is never reused, and evidence you log by hand counts toward conviction reviews.

**3. Readable references.** "AAPL.p1" now reads "AAPL pillar 1", "NVDA.p2.supports" reads "NVDA pillar 2 · Supports", "synthetic_000197" reads "Email 197", and assumption IDs show their names. Messages and reasons are rewritten the same way. Log entry IDs and criteria version codes are no longer shown, and the code-style chips are plain text now.

**4. Positions in dollars and shares.** These use a synthetic $1bn fund and synthetic reference prices, so 300 bps becomes $30.0m, or 166,667 NVDA shares at a synthetic $180. Both figures are stored once in `thresholds.py` and labeled synthetic on the page; the book itself still stores basis points.

**5. Logos.** The five public-domain SVGs you approved replace the ticker squares across the Book, company pages, review and suggestions.

**6. Book table.** The Conviction column is gone. Each row now ends with a **Full research log** button, which opens an "Example — not connected yet" placeholder page.

"Needs a person" now reads **"Attention required"** everywhere, including the Eval measure names. The Needs attention tab and its pills keep their name, since you asked about "Needs a person" specifically; say if you want those changed too.

Everything since the last commit is uncommitted on the `ui-polish` branch. This round's choices are recorded in `DECISIONS.md` #63–66. Should I commit it there?

# human_attention

## Definition
The email contains a request, meeting or opportunity that needs a person on the desk to act, from a credible and valuable source: for example a small-group meeting with company management, a call offered by a differentiated analyst or industry expert, or a time-sensitive question from a trusted contact. All sales invitations, generic networking requests and mass conference promotions do not qualify.

## Rules
- R1: Answer yes only when the email asks the desk to do something specific, such as accept a meeting, join a call or reply to a question.
- R2: Answer no for well-known conferences, broad investor days and routine analyst meetings that offer no small-group or differentiated access.
- R3: Answer no when the email only shares information or research, however useful, and asks for no action.
- R4: A deadline or urgent subject line alone does not make a request qualify; judge the access offered and the source's role.

All five changes are in, with tests for each. The full suite passes, `mypy --strict` is clean, and I checked Needs attention and the Book in the browser.

**1. Analyst rating placeholders.**
- Each stock now shows an **Analyst rating** slot: a column in the Book table, a badge on each thesis card, and a card on the company page. Until the data arrives it shows a greyed Buy · Hold · Sell marked **Pending**.
- Each company page also has a **Suggested rating changes**, tagged **Upcoming**. It explains that suggested moves toward buy or toward sell, with the emails behind them, will appear there.
- The other session confirmed the backend fields: `street_view` (buy/hold/sell) and `street_view_note`. Once its branch merges, real ratings replace the placeholders with no further UI work. Wiring the panel to its suggested-rating-change field is a small job after that.

**2. Inbox placement.** Inbox now sits directly right of Morning brief, with a light green tint. The order is Morning brief · Inbox · Review · Needs attention · Book.

**3. Ordering meeting requests.** Needs attention has an **Order by** toggle:
- **Time** (default): soonest reply first, grouped by Due today / Tomorrow / Later.
- **Relevance**: ranked by the classifier's attention score.
- **Ticker relevance**: ranked by how strongly each request bears on a covered company.

The scores only set the order; none is shown.

**4. Respond / Reject.** Each request card has **Respond** and **Reject**. Either one clears the request from the queue, the brief's attention list and the tab count. It then sits in a collapsed **Handled this session** group with **Undo**. No email is ever sent, and the buttons say so. This lasts for the session, and Reset clears it.

**5. Street target price.** It's a **Street target** column in the Book and part of the target-price card on each company page, showing **Pending** for now. The other session has added `street_target_price` and is filling it for all five companies (within about ±10% of the consensus-implied target). I relabelled the existing consensus figure **consensus-implied**, since it's computed from consensus assumptions rather than being the Street's published target.

These are in `DECISIONS.md` (#74–76). I told the other session I'd taken those numbers, so it will start at 77. This round is uncommitted on `ui-polish`.

def earlier_email(record: RedundancyRecord | None, data: DayData) -> Email | None:
    """The nearest earlier email of a flagged repeat, as extraction context; never a quarantined one."""
    if record is None or not record.flagged or record.nearest is None or data.quarantined(record.nearest):
        return None
    return data.emails.get(record.nearest)

[2026-10-09T09:06:09] ASSISTANT
The Inbox now has tag filtering in the search bar and a two-way order toggle. All tests pass, `mypy --strict` is clean, and I tried it in the browser.

**Order by: Time received or Relevance.** The toggle has just these two. Relevance uses the hidden signal score to order the list without showing it. The Ticker, Category and Topic groupings are gone, since tags cover them.

**Tags in the search bar.** There are three ways to add one:
- Type a tag (a ticker like **AAPL**, a category like **Actionable** or **Worth watching**, or a topic like **Macro**) and press **Enter**, or pick it from the suggestions that appear while typing.
- Click one of the ticker buttons (**AMZN NVDA MSFT AAPL GOOGL**) under the search bar.
- Click any tag on an email row (its category, tickers or topics).

Each tag becomes a green chip in the search bar; remove it with its **×**, or with Backspace in an empty search box. Emails must match every chip, plus any free text you type and the All / Passed / Needs attention sub-tab. The old label dropdown is gone, since the chips replace it.

I tested this live: AAPL alone left 26 emails, all tagged AAPL. Adding "Worth watching" from a row narrowed it to 12, and removing AAPL left the 60 Worth-watching emails.

Each preset shows its one-line note on the Live page, so you can pick by the outcome you want to demo.

**How I chose them:**
- **Real corpus emails.** I didn't write any: the repo's rules say emails are never generated, so these are existing corpus emails.
- **Day 2, not day 1.** The app compares live runs against day 1, so a day-1 email would just match itself as a repeat.
- **Pipeline results, not corpus labels.** I picked using the pipeline's own day-2 results, so the corpus labels still never touch the app.

**Before you demo:**
- **The first run of each preset costs real tokens.** The new presets aren't cached yet. The other session reported its OpenRouter credits ran out during its last run, so a first run may fail until credits are topped up.
- **Live results can differ from the notes.** The notes describe day 2's batch results. The classifier criteria and gate changed since (for example, "Worth watching" now needs a 0.70 score), so a live run can come out differently.

Recorded as `DECISIONS.md` #121. I told the other session about the change, since it also works with the data folder. The preset swap is staged and everything else is uncommitted, all on `ui-polish`.

    def spend(v: View) -> str | None:
        """Take one live run from the session's and the process's hourly allowance; the reason when refused."""
        if v.sess.runs_left() <= 0:
            return f"This session has used its {thresholds.LIVE_RUNS_PER_HOUR} live runs for the hour."
        if not global_runs.take():
            return (f"The app has used its {thresholds.LIVE_RUNS_PER_HOUR_GLOBAL} live runs for the hour "
                    "across all visitors. Try again later.")
        v.sess.take_run()
        return None

    def refund(v: View) -> None:
        if v.sess.live_runs:
            v.sess.live_runs.pop()
        global_runs.refund()

def test_live_input_cap_and_rate_limit(client: TestClient, fake_stages: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    big = "x" * (thresholds.LIVE_INPUT_CAP_CHARS + 1)
    assert client.post("/live", headers=HX, data={"body": big}).status_code == 413
    assert client.post("/live", headers=HX, data={"body": ""}).status_code == 422
    codes = [client.post("/live", headers=HX, data={"body": f"note {i}"}).status_code
             for i in range(thresholds.LIVE_RUNS_PER_HOUR + 1)]
    assert codes == [200] * thresholds.LIVE_RUNS_PER_HOUR + [429]
